# Red wine

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="red-wine-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="red-wine-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="red-wine-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Red wine in the HMI library has 53 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, antimony, uranium and V from 6 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a <ul>
<li>
<strong>Metals measured:</strong>

Lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury and nickel and others, in 53 results from 6 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Centre for Food Safety (Hong Kong) · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants">The First Hong Kong Total Diet Study: Metallic Contaminants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Centre for Food Safety, Food and Environmental Hygiene Department, Government of the Hong Kong Special Administrative Region</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Main drivers:</strong>

Form, basis and where the red wine was sampled; reporting limits decide how many results are non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Centre for Food Safety (Hong Kong) · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants">The First Hong Kong Total Diet Study: Metallic Contaminants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Centre for Food Safety, Food and Environmental Hygiene Department, Government of the Hong Kong Special Administrative Region</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Practical framing:</strong>

Ask for lot results with a quantification limit well below the limit that applies in the destination market.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Centre for Food Safety (Hong Kong) · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants">The First Hong Kong Total Diet Study: Metallic Contaminants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Centre for Food Safety, Food and Environmental Hygiene Department, Government of the Hong Kong Special Administrative Region</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

HKSAR · 2012</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfs2012-hktds-inorganic-arsenic">The First Hong Kong Total Diet Study: Inorganic Arsenic</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span href="../sources/cfs2012-hktds-inorganic-arsenic">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported lead in red wine, as sold from 3,025 results in 3 reporting regions, with subgroup medians from 4.68 to 10 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> In the US Total Diet Study, red wine, as sold had a median lead result of 4.4 µg/kg across 3 composites from 2018–2020.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Spungen et al. 2024 reported lead at 5.3 µg/kg (mean) in red wine, as sold from all samples.<sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Centre for Food Safety FEHD HKSAR 2012 reported inorganic arsenic at 5 µg/kg (mean) in red wine, as sold from all samples (4 sampling occasions, composites).<sup class="cite">[[sources/cfs2012-hktds-inorganic-arsenic|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Centre for Food Safety FEHD HKSAR · 2012</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfs2012-hktds-inorganic-arsenic">The First Hong Kong Total Diet Study: Inorganic Arsenic</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Centre for Food Safety, Food and Environmental Hygiene Department, Government of the Hong Kong Special Administrative Region</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfs2012-hktds-inorganic-arsenic">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the red wine came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-red-wine" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-red-wine-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of red wine, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

Red wine, as sold; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>≤4.4</td><td>4.4–7.25 (bounded)</td><td>7.1–16.5 (bounded)</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>≤4.4</td><td>4.4–7.25 (bounded)</td><td>7.1–12.9 (bounded)</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>37 reference units; 2 sampling families. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>34</td><td>50%</td></tr><tr><td>FDA Total Diet Study</td><td>3</td><td>50%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for red wine, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>2 sampling families with different designs; none is a probability sample of world supply. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li><li><a href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" class="internal" data-slug="sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" data-no-cite-meta>FDA Total Diet Study</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Method: hmi-pooled-reference-v2.</p><p>hmi-pooled-reference-v2:red-wine-c14b74578399:d6ecc530e8d1</p><p>The reference is computed by HMI from admitted evidence, not copied from a study baseline. It describes the evidence currently in this scope; it is not a worldwide or current-lot benchmark. Source, year and region filters do not change it. Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div></details><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-baseline-fallback id="hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:red-wine-3f2e7913422f:30ed443fde02"><summary>HMI red wine Cd reference · Red wine, as sold</summary><div data-baseline-reference-template="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:red-wine-3f2e7913422f:30ed443fde02"><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Reference and scope</p><p>HMI red wine Cd reference · Red wine, as sold. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>FDA Total Diet Study</td><td>3</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for red wine, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>FDA Total Diet Study is the only compatible sampling family for red wine, as sold on this basis in the completed red wine ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" class="internal" data-slug="sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" data-no-cite-meta>FDA Total Diet Study</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Basis: As sold, as eaten or fresh weight.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p></div><div class="hmi-selected-comparison" data-selected-comparison><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">HMI baseline position</p><p>Limited data · HMI red wine Pb reference · Red wine, as sold. median as reported: ≤15 µg/kg. HMI median 4.4–7.25 (bounded); Q1 ≤4.4; Q3 7.1–16.5 (bounded) µg/kg. 2 families. Reporting bounds prevent a rating.</p><p>Reporting bounds or insufficient reference spread prevent a stable Low / Moderate / High classification.</p><p>This finding’s sampling family contributes to the fixed reference.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sensitivity to weighting</p><p>Equal weight per subgroup: Limited data. The position remains unresolved under this alternative. This check does not address all sampling, rounding or analytical uncertainty.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sensitivity to source selection</p><ul><li>Without WHO GEMS/Food: Limited data.</li><li>Without FDA Total Diet Study: Limited data.</li></ul><p>These checks show dependence on each sampling family; they are not confidence intervals.</p><div data-baseline-reference="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:red-wine-c14b74578399:d6ecc530e8d1"><a href="#hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:red-wine-c14b74578399:d6ecc530e8d1">Reference, contributing evidence and methods</a></div></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Collection period</p><p data-timeline-scope>2003–2020</p><div data-selected-timeline><p>No year-by-year sample register is reconstructed from this summary.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Geographic scope</p><p data-regional-scope>EURO</p><div data-selected-regional><p>Purchase or reporting market; growing area not identified.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sampling, analysis and reporting limits</p><p>As described by the monitoring programme.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p><p></p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Shared samples and study overlap</p><p>Subgroup rows from the same source share its laboratory and sampling frame; they are not independent studies.</p><p>Each table row describes a food/analyte result. Lot testing against the applicable limit is the check that can be verified.

<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>

<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 0.5 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 200 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup References identify the sources used in these answers. The Sources index preserves additional discovery links; inclusion there does not mean a source has been reviewed for this assessment. Corrections, completed searches, new direct evidence and changes in requirements should reopen the relevant answer.


<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Red wine FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-red-wine" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-red-wine" data-question="What heavy metals are found in red wine?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in red wine?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, antimony, uranium and V in red wine, most often lead, cadmium and total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Centre for Food Safety (Hong Kong) · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants">The First Hong Kong Total Diet Study: Metallic Contaminants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Centre for Food Safety, Food and Environmental Hygiene Department, Government of the Hong Kong Special Administrative Region</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfs2013-hktds-metallic-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported lead in red wine, as sold from 3,025 results in 3 reporting regions, with subgroup medians from 4.68 to 10 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-red-wine" data-anchor="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-red-wine" data-question="Which regions report the lowest levels in red wine?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Which regions report the lowest levels in red wine?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Among GEMS/Food reporting regions, red wine, as sold had the lowest lead subgroup median in PAHO (7.4 µg/kg) and the highest in WPRO (10 µg/kg); reporting regions are markets, not growing areas.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-red-wine-is-inorganic" data-anchor="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-red-wine-is-inorganic" data-question="How much of the arsenic in red wine is inorganic?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How much of the arsenic in red wine is inorganic?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Inorganic arsenic, the more toxic form, was measured in 3 results from 2 sources, far fewer than total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/cfs2012-hktds-inorganic-arsenic|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Centre for Food Safety FEHD HKSAR · 2012</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfs2012-hktds-inorganic-arsenic">The First Hong Kong Total Diet Study: Inorganic Arsenic</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Centre for Food Safety, Food and Environmental Hygiene Department, Government of the Hong Kong Special Administrative Region</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfs2012-hktds-inorganic-arsenic">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Centre for Food Safety FEHD HKSAR 2012 reported inorganic arsenic at 5 µg/kg (mean) in red wine, as sold from all samples (4 sampling occasions, composites).<sup class="cite">[[sources/cfs2012-hktds-inorganic-arsenic|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Centre for Food Safety FEHD HKSAR · 2012</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cfs2012-hktds-inorganic-arsenic">The First Hong Kong Total Diet Study: Inorganic Arsenic</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Centre for Food Safety, Food and Environmental Hygiene Department, Government of the Hong Kong Special Administrative Region</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cfs2012-hktds-inorganic-arsenic">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
